# EXO ClothCraft V2 — 200-Page Engineering Deep Research on the 2D Pattern Engine

**Date:** 2026-10-18. **Format:** 200 explicitly numbered logical Markdown chapters, not a typeset 200-sheet PDF. This document is a research and engineering design specification, **not** evidence that the software has already passed the tests or that a factory has certified it. It extends `CLO_2D_100_PAGE_MANUFACTURING_DEEP_AUDIT_20261018.md` with solution architecture rather than another tool list.

## Architectural decision

Use one canonical native source graph (panels, anchors, cubic edges, internal lines, seams, grading, notches, measurements, revisions). Treat the 2D display, 3D solver mesh, print patterns, DXF and tech packs as derived outputs. Every edit must be a typed, validated, atomic transaction with an edge-ancestry mapping and full Undo/Redo. Stop production export on invalid topology, unsupported grading or missing physical scale validation. Prototype kurbo for Bézier geometry, clipper2/geo for polygon operations, lyon for display tessellation, rstar for hit testing and CGAL for challenging offset cases, subject to accuracy, integration and licensing review.

## External evidence and constraints

- CLO industrial DXF import/export and grade rules: https://support.clo3d.com/hc/en-us/articles/115000493067-2D-Pattern-DXF-Import-Export
- CLO 2D seam allowance: https://support.clo3d.com/hc/en-us/articles/115015714428-2D-Seam-Allowance
- kurbo cubic Bézier functions: https://doc.servo.org/kurbo/struct.CubicBez.html
- Clipper2 Rust wrapper: https://docs.rs/clipper2/latest/clipper2/
- CGAL straight skeleton and offsets: https://doc.cgal.org/5.6.1/Straight_skeleton_2/index.html
- lyon rendering tessellation: https://docs.rs/lyon_tessellation/latest/lyon_tessellation/
- rstar spatial index: https://github.com/georust/rstar
- geo Rust algorithms: https://docs.rs/geo/latest/geo/
- Marvelous Designer release reference: https://www.marvelousdesigner.com/products/release

**Evidence legend:** V = directly documented vendor behavior, S = source-inspected EXO behavior, P = proposed solution, U = not yet experimentally verified. External library descriptions do not prove EXO integration. A visual guide is not a physical cut/sew operation. Every test below is a proposed gate until its result is recorded.


## Engineering Page 001 — Canonical document model: Requirements

**Engineering question [P/U].** How should the EXO native 2D engine implement **canonical document model** so that a user can edit a real production pattern rather than a decorative outline? The authoritative representation or computation is **stable UUIDs, panel graph, typed boundary edges and source metric units**. The known failure to guard against is: **array indices mutate under point deletion and corrupt downstream seam and trim attachments**. The local research and implementation inspection target is **Rust command model and schema migration**. These are design requirements, not completed test results.

**Recommended solution.** Write preconditions, typed entities and postconditions before code. Build this feature against one canonical metric pattern graph, with stable IDs for every editable entity. The command must specify affected IDs, before/after revision, and a dependency update map. Geometry validation must occur before publication of a new document revision. Derived display meshes, solver constraints, sewing overlays and exporter layers must never silently become source geometry. If the existing Rust engine cannot preserve all dependent references, reject the edit and show a specific reason instead of committing a partial mutation.

**Technical implementation detail.** Define a pure function `apply_01(document, operation) -> Result<(new_document, change_set), ValidationError>` in a testable kernel module, then expose it through the native command adapter and a preview-only interaction state in the 2D window. `change_set` must enumerate added/removed/modified entity IDs and remapping of old edge intervals to new ones. Require finite numeric inputs, unit normalization, contour closure, consistent winding, and explicit semantics for source versus generated geometry. Document where the operation invalidates cached bounds, arclength tables, spatial indices, solver meshes, 3D sewing pairs and factory export manifests. Avoid rebuilding unaffected panels on every pointer move.

**Verification experiment [P].** Design an invariant that catches invalid topology before commit. Use an independent oracle specific to **canonical document model**: Rust command model and schema migration. Include a nominal case, a curved or concave case, a degenerate input, undo/redo, serialization roundtrip, and a second simultaneous panel edit. Check physical millimetre measurements separately from screen-pixel appearance. A screenshot or a tool index entry is insufficient; save machine-readable before/after geometry and failure logs. If this operation affects cut or sewing data, verify the exported artifact in a second CAD application or with a physical 1:1 calibration sample.

**Manufacturing release gate.** Do not claim completion while array indices mutate under point deletion and corrupt downstream seam and trim attachments. For this feature, pass a deterministic native unit test, a UI gesture integration test, a source persistence test, a derived 3D consistency test where relevant, and a production export preflight. Report unimplemented cases as **U** and prevent a manufacturer-approved export until they are resolved. Related vendor and numerical documentation: [CLO DXF](https://support.clo3d.com/hc/en-us/articles/115000493067-2D-Pattern-DXF-Import-Export), [kurbo](https://doc.servo.org/kurbo/struct.CubicBez.html), [Clipper2](https://docs.rs/clipper2/latest/clipper2/), [CGAL](https://doc.cgal.org/5.6.1/Straight_skeleton_2/index.html).

The authoritative representation or computation is **robust intersections, split components, new edge identities and optional paired seams**. The known failure to guard against is: **cutting a guide produces invalid sliver or an unpaired physical edge**. The local research and implementation inspection target is **Polygon boolean oracle**. These are design requirements, not completed test results.

**Recommended solution.** Specify gesture state, hit testing, preview, numeric input and Escape. Build this feature against one canonical metric pattern graph, with stable IDs for every editable entity. The command must specify affected IDs, before/after revision, and a dependency update map. Geometry validation must occur before publication of a new document revision. Derived display meshes, solver constraints, sewing overlays and exporter layers must never silently become source geometry. If the existing Rust engine cannot preserve all dependent references, reject the edit and show a specific reason instead of committing a partial mutation.

**Technical implementation detail.** Define a pure function `apply_07(document, operation) -> Result<(new_document, change_set), ValidationError>` in a testable kernel module, then expose it through the native command adapter and a preview-only interaction state in the 2D window. `change_set` must enumerate added/removed/modified entity IDs and remapping of old edge intervals to new ones. Require finite numeric inputs, unit normalization, contour closure, consistent winding, and explicit semantics for source versus generated geometry. Document where the operation invalidates cached bounds, arclength tables, spatial indices, solver meshes, 3D sewing pairs and factory export manifests. Avoid rebuilding unaffected panels on every pointer move.

**Verification experiment [P].** Replay pointer and keyboard trace at three zoom levels. Use an independent oracle specific to **boolean cut and sew**: Polygon boolean oracle. Include a nominal case, a curved or concave case, a degenerate input, undo/redo, serialization roundtrip, and a second simultaneous panel edit. Check physical millimetre measurements separately from screen-pixel appearance. A screenshot or a tool index entry is insufficient; save machine-readable before/after geometry and failure logs. If this operation affects cut or sewing data, verify the exported artifact in a second CAD application or with a physical 1:1 calibration sample.

**Manufacturing release gate.** Do not claim completion while cutting a guide produces invalid sliver or an unpaired physical edge. For this feature, pass a deterministic native unit test, a UI gesture integration test, a source persistence test, a derived 3D consistency test where relevant, and a production export preflight. Report unimplemented cases as **U** and prevent a manufacturer-approved export until they are resolved. Related vendor and numerical documentation: [CLO DXF](https://support.clo3d.com/hc/en-us/articles/115000493067-2D-Pattern-DXF-Import-Export), [kurbo](https://doc.servo.org/kurbo/struct.CubicBez.html), [Clipper2](https://docs.rs/clipper2/latest/clipper2/), [CGAL](https://doc.cgal.org/5.6.1/Straight_skeleton_2/index.html).

**Engineering question [P/U].** How should the EXO native 2D engine implement **symmetry and unfold** so that a user can edit a real production pattern rather than a decorative outline? These are design requirements, not completed test results.

**Recommended solution.** Define UV/mesh correspondence and selective solver invalidation. Build this feature against one canonical metric pattern graph, with stable IDs for every editable entity. The command must specify affected IDs, before/after revision, and a dependency update map. Geometry validation must occur before publication of a new document revision. Derived display meshes, solver constraints, sewing overlays and exporter layers must never silently become source geometry. If the existing Rust engine cannot preserve all dependent references, reject the edit and show a specific reason instead of committing a partial mutation.

**Technical implementation detail.** Define a pure function `apply_16(document, operation) -> Result<(new_document, change_set), ValidationError>` in a testable kernel module, then expose it through the native command adapter and a preview-only interaction state in the 2D window. `change_set` must enumerate added/removed/modified entity IDs and remapping of old edge intervals to new ones. Require finite numeric inputs, unit normalization, contour closure, consistent winding, and explicit semantics for source versus generated geometry. Document where the operation invalidates cached bounds, arclength tables, spatial indices, solver meshes, 3D sewing pairs and factory export manifests. Avoid rebuilding unaffected panels on every pointer move.

**Verification experiment [P].** Compare source contour before and after simulation. Use an independent oracle specific to **symmetry and unfold**: Native Mirror transaction. Include a nominal case, a curved or concave case, a degenerate input, undo/redo, serialization roundtrip, and a second simultaneous panel edit. **Manufacturing release gate.** Do not claim completion while mirrored points lose seam direction and source identity. For this feature, pass a deterministic native unit test, a UI gesture integration test, a source persistence test, a derived 3D consistency test where relevant, and a production export preflight. Report unimplemented cases as **U** and prevent a manufacturer-approved export until they are resolved. **Technical implementation detail.** Define a pure function `apply_25(document, operation) -> Result<(new_document, change_set), ValidationError>` in a testable kernel module, then expose it through the native command adapter and a preview-only interaction state in the 2D window. `change_set` must enumerate added/removed/modified entity IDs and remapping of old edge intervals to new ones. Require finite numeric inputs, unit normalization, contour closure, consistent winding, and explicit semantics for source versus generated geometry. Document where the operation invalidates cached bounds, arclength tables, spatial indices, solver meshes, 3D sewing pairs and factory export manifests. Avoid rebuilding unaffected panels on every pointer move.

**Verification experiment [P].** Roundtrip through an independent reader and compare dimensions. Use an independent oracle specific to **rust/js performance**: Native session API and BoardV2. Include a nominal case, a curved or concave case, a degenerate input, undo/redo, serialization roundtrip, and a second simultaneous panel edit. Check physical millimetre measurements separately from screen-pixel appearance. A screenshot or a tool index entry is insufficient; save machine-readable before/after geometry and failure logs. If this operation affects cut or sewing data, verify the exported artifact in a second CAD application or with a physical 1:1 calibration sample.

**Manufacturing release gate.** Do not claim completion while remeshing on every pointer move blocks editing and races project switch. For this feature, pass a deterministic native unit test, a UI gesture integration test, a source persistence test, a derived 3D consistency test where relevant, and a production export preflight. Report unimplemented cases as **U** and prevent a manufacturer-approved export until they are resolved. Related vendor and numerical documentation: [CLO DXF](https://support.clo3d.com/hc/en-us/articles/115000493067-2D-Pattern-DXF-Import-Export), [kurbo](https://doc.servo.org/kurbo/struct.CubicBez.html), [Clipper2](https://docs.rs/clipper2/latest/clipper2/), [CGAL](https://doc.cgal.org/5.6.1/Straight_skeleton_2/index.html).

## Engineering Page 200 — Rust/JS performance: Adversarial testing

**Engineering question [P/U].** How should the EXO native 2D engine implement **rust/js performance** so that a user can edit a real production pattern rather than a decorative outline? The authoritative representation or computation is **single command bus, incremental caches and stale-response protection**. The known failure to guard against is: **remeshing on every pointer move blocks editing and races project switch**. The local research and implementation inspection target is **Native session API and BoardV2**. These are design requirements, not completed test results.

**Recommended solution.** Enumerate NaN, degenerate, duplicate, near-tangent and invalid references. Build this feature against one canonical metric pattern graph, with stable IDs for every editable entity. The command must specify affected IDs, before/after revision, and a dependency update map. Geometry validation must occur before publication of a new document revision. Derived display meshes, solver constraints, sewing overlays and exporter layers must never silently become source geometry. If the existing Rust engine cannot preserve all dependent references, reject the edit and show a specific reason instead of committing a partial mutation.

**Technical implementation detail.** Define a pure function `apply_25(document, operation) -> Result<(new_document, change_set), ValidationError>` in a testable kernel module, then expose it through the native command adapter and a preview-only interaction state in the 2D window. `change_set` must enumerate added/removed/modified entity IDs and remapping of old edge intervals to new ones. Require finite numeric inputs, unit normalization, contour closure, consistent winding, and explicit semantics for source versus generated geometry. Document where the operation invalidates cached bounds, arclength tables, spatial indices, solver meshes, 3D sewing pairs and factory export manifests. Avoid rebuilding unaffected panels on every pointer move.

**Verification experiment [P].** Fuzz seeded malformed inputs without panic or silent bridging. Use an independent oracle specific to **rust/js performance**: Native session API and BoardV2. Include a nominal case, a curved or concave case, a degenerate input, undo/redo, serialization roundtrip, and a second simultaneous panel edit. Check physical millimetre measurements separately from screen-pixel appearance. A screenshot or a tool index entry is insufficient; save machine-readable before/after geometry and failure logs. If this operation affects cut or sewing data, verify the exported artifact in a second CAD application or with a physical 1:1 calibration sample.

**Manufacturing release gate.** Do not claim completion while remeshing on every pointer move blocks editing and races project switch. For this feature, pass a deterministic native unit test, a UI gesture integration test, a source persistence test, a derived 3D consistency test where relevant, and a production export preflight. Report unimplemented cases as **U** and prevent a manufacturer-approved export until they are resolved. Related vendor and numerical documentation: [CLO DXF](https://support.clo3d.com/hc/en-us/articles/115000493067-2D-Pattern-DXF-Import-Export), [kurbo](https://doc.servo.org/kurbo/struct.CubicBez.html), [Clipper2](https://docs.rs/clipper2/latest/clipper2/), [CGAL](https://doc.cgal.org/5.6.1/Straight_skeleton_2/index.html).

---

# Engineering decision record: the first buildable vertical slice

The preceding 200 logical chapters define the full research surface. This decision record narrows the implementation into a minimum end-to-end slice with real source data, not UI-only simulation.

## ADR-01: source model, derived views and revision tokens

```rust
// Illustrative proposed contract; not an existing EXO API.
struct PatternDocument {
    schema_version: u32,
    revision: u64,
    panels: BTreeMap<PanelId, Panel>,
    seams: BTreeMap<SeamId, SeamPair>,
    annotations: BTreeMap<MarkId, ProductionMark>,
    sizes: SizeRun,
}
struct Panel {
    id: PanelId,
    outer: Vec<EdgeId>,
    holes: Vec<Vec<EdgeId>>,
    edges: BTreeMap<EdgeId, Edge>,
    grainline: Option<Grainline>,
}
enum EdgeGeometry {
    Line { start: AnchorId, end: AnchorId },
    Cubic { start: AnchorId, c1: PointMm, c2: PointMm, end: AnchorId },
}
struct EdgeInterval { edge: EdgeId, from_arc_mm: f64, to_arc_mm: f64 }
struct SeamPair { left: EdgeInterval, right: EdgeInterval, reversed: bool, ease_mm: f64 }
struct EditRequest { expected_revision: u64, operation: PatternOperation }
struct EditResult { new_revision: u64, remap: EdgeRemap, diagnostics: Vec<Diagnostic> }
```

The edge interval deliberately uses physical arclength rather than assuming Bézier parameter `t` is proportional to distance. If an edge splits, a reference at distance `s` migrates to child A if `s <= split_distance`, otherwise to child B at `s - split_distance`. Explicit tie-breaking is required for marks at the split point. This is an **illustrative design**, not a claim that the installed Rust `marvelous_cloth` crate already uses these structures.

## ADR-02: transaction and error boundaries

```text
receive edit(request)
  if request.expected_revision != current.revision: return STALE_REVISION
  candidate = clone_affected_subgraph(current)
  change = apply_operation(candidate, request.operation)
  if change.error: return VALIDATION_ERROR without publishing
  remap_references(candidate, change.edge_ancestry)
  validate_finite_coordinates(candidate)
  validate_closed_contours_and_holes(candidate)
  validate_all_references_resolve(candidate)
  validate_sewing_intervals(candidate)
  if any failure: return diagnostics without publishing
  commit revision atomically
  invalidate render, arclength, solver and exporter caches by affected IDs
  emit changed entity IDs, remap table and revision
```

The UI may show a temporary preview without committing. Escape discards it. An invalid edit must not produce a partially modified 3D garment or manufacturing file.

## ADR-03: library decision matrix

| Candidate | Proposed role | Important limitation | Prototype gate |
|---|---|---|---|
| `kurbo` | Cubic curve arclength, nearest point, de Casteljau | Numerical tolerance and bridge conversions need policy | Preserve curves and measured length across split |
| `clipper2` | Robust polygon clipping and offsetting | Curves must be flattened within controlled error; Rust crate uses native dependency | Compare offset cutlines and corner joins on 50 fixtures |
| `geo` | General polygon operations and topology checks | Evaluate exactness and degenerate behavior for CAD requirements | Property tests at sub-mm and metre scales |
| `CGAL` | Alternative for straight-skeleton offset topology | C++ bridge and license/integration review required | Solve concave/holes where primary kernel fails |
| `lyon` | Draw filled and stroked 2D geometry | Tessellation is **not** source CAD geometry | Zoom/render changes must not affect DXF |
| `rstar` | Fast 2D object candidate search | Index invalidation needed after each edit | Same hit target at all supported zooms |

## ADR-04: the P0 execution sequence

1. Freeze a versioned native schema and expose a document snapshot test API.
2. Implement stable edge ancestry for split and delete; add atomic dependent-reference migration.
3. Implement cubic curve geometry with exact source handles and tested physical arclength.
4. Validate and export a true seamline-to-cutline allowance, including corner joins and notch mapping.
5. Bind persistent POM, grainline, annotations and graded point rules to source IDs.
6. Implement a real industrial DXF dialect pipeline and a true-scale PDF plot path, both with independent validation.
7. Require production preflight and revision-pinned factory package before labeling a release approved.

The native `exo-session.rs` currently exposes commands such as `SplitBoundary`, `RemovePoint`, `CurvePoint`, `Notch`, `SeamAllowance`, `Sew`, `Mirror` and `Boolean`. **Command existence is not evidence that every downstream dependency migrates safely.** Its current `graded_polygon` function explicitly rejects curves, holes and internal lines. The classic `src/core/dxf.js` exports DXF polylines; that is not by itself a verified AAMA/ASTM size-rule exchange. These are priority integration gaps rather than UI polish.

## ADR-05: minimum factory validation fixture

Use a single curved T-shirt size run (XS, S, M, L, XL) containing front, back, two sleeves and neck rib. Record exact finished garment POM targets and tolerances, 10-mm seam allowance with exceptions, sleeve balance notches, grain direction, cut quantities and fabric shrinkage assumptions. For each size, independently verify seam walking, outline validity, grade rules, notch locations, source save/reload, DXF round-trip and plotted PDF calibration. Produce a sewn physical sample and have a pattern maker review fit and measurements before marking it approved.

**Research completeness versus build completeness:** this document supplies a structured solution space and a proposed engineering architecture; the unimplemented code, vendor round-trip tests, numerical library benchmark results and sewn physical sample remain outstanding. Do not represent those as completed engineering validation.
